ParlaMint-AT_2010-06-17-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00070_d8e2600	We arrive at the 1st. Question No 71, by Mr Mayer to the Federal Minister for Education, Arts and Culture Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_2010-06-17-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00070_d8e2619	Madam President. In the three and a half years of her previous term of office, Mrs Bundesminister, you have launched a great deal of work in terms of school and education policy, have been very committed to the “new school” and are still fighting. Some examples of this: smaller classes, New secondary school, introduction of education standards, new matura for AHS, and now it comes in for the BHS. So a lot has been brought up by you. Even this afternoon, when it comes to the 2010 educational package, we will again put a few things in this direction on the rails. It would be important – also for the spectators at home and at home – to know the following: 71/M “What are the key points of the overall education policy approach?” I know it's difficult to present all this in two minutes, but I think it's important that we get an insight into your overall concept with the milestones you've set.
ParlaMint-AT_2010-06-17-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00070_d8e2652	Mrs Bundesminister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2010-06-17-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00070_d8e2667	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen! Ladies and gentlemen! The goal is clear: Education is the key to success! The answer in two minutes is indeed an ambitious undertaking, but I will try to present a few corner points. It must be central for the public education system to rely on quality and performance. That is the central point of approach! Especially when we look at the situation in the big cities of our country, we see that education must also make an important contribution to integration. People with immigration history must be particularly focused on education policy, especially since most of the population are socially disadvantaged. That's what I want to do. The prosperity of Austria is decided in the classroom. I would therefore like to focus on school and outline three key points. Corner point one: Quality and performance, all the measures such as smaller classes, new matura, education standards, new middle school. Second very central, important area: Teachers. We need a new education and training for teachers. In this respect, we will be ready for decision at the end of the year. The concepts will be there. Of course, we also need a new right of service and remuneration. It was only yesterday that we had very constructive talks with the representatives of the teachers' union. And the third major issue – Mrs Haubner will also address him – is the school administration. There is a need to streamline the public authorities. And my big goal is the self-responsible school, the independent school. This means more personal responsibility at the location, but also air and free space for breathing for the schools.

We know that in connection with new advertising windows of Austrian daughters of numerous German media companies, around € 280 million of advertising revenue goes to these operators alone. The independent media authority has the task of evaluating at the end of the day and then freeing the money. That is, we give an additional signal that money we provide by law will be dealt with responsibly. That's a very central point. It's not about giving anything. It is also crucial to take into account the quality of the programme, the efficiency of the company and the efficiency of the use of the funds. I think that will be possible with this decision. In addition, the ORF is federally organized, i.e. we have nine national studios. There are rightly nine national studios because it is important to be close to regional identities and citizens. To a few more specific points in this law: The ORF receives, through this law, €160 million from taxpayers, distributed over four years, in four tranches. This is not a matter of course. Of course, it can be justified by the failure by the exemption from fees; I justify it with a second fact, namely that the ORF, coming from the monopoly, has now come into a competitive situation with cost structures out of this monopoly situation, which is not forward-looking in the future. This is a challenge for the management and for the institutions of the company – no question – and we want to help them with this bridge money to advance and finance the necessary structural reforms in the company. But it must also be clear that these degressive payments will end in four years. The particular challenge for the company and its management is to use this money to make the company fit so that it can balance in four years without these state grants and is therefore economically viable. This is a special challenge and a special commitment. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] We have also given the whole of a strict control. Some people in the company may not like this, but I think it is absolutely necessary and now accepted by everyone in the sense of careful handling of tax money. We are able by this law to install a media authority that will be able to monitor independently of the company's public service mandate and, of course, also in the special case about the careful use of the taxpayers' money. I would like to draw attention to two particular points which have been very controversial in the last few days: The ORF undoubtedly also has an excellent offer in the online field, but of course it is not a matter of course that a public-law media company with fee financing has done as much as the ORF has done well, and may also move away from the programme support. I know that some still lack the understanding that this is simply and simply necessary in the balance between private and public law, but it is necessary. We also manage to consider the private broadcasters with a promotion or even to increase the funding we introduced last year. That should also be mentioned. This package of laws includes not only the ORF law, although it is always in the foreground, but it includes in total eight laws, including a federal constitution law. This Federal Constitutional Act is the reason why the discussion about the two-thirds majority has now been initiated, because it is intended to create for the first time a constitutionally independent media authority. In the ORF this was not the case, and it is now at least partially understood. But we have linked this so-called fee-refunding to various points, which are particularly important for the spectators, spectators, listeners and seers. One point is, for example, the creation of a new information and cultural channel. We have negotiated this in the government programme, we have now, as it were, also established in the law. It is also exactly the time when this is to be created. A second point is that the sports channel must be maintained. It is because there have been discussions with sports organisations over and over again, in addition to being fixed, that current sports reporting should also take place on this sports channel, and that reporting should not be made dependent on it or linked to the condition that a production cost grant is paid for it. An agreement has been reached with the filmmakers that it is continued and even expanded. Not only is it about making 5 or 8 million € available from the ORF for cinema productions now, but if this would not be done by the ORF – private sponsors who do this – there are relatively few in Austria – the film promotion would collapse overall, and that would be at a time when we have in Hollywood Oscar hires gene and Oscar participations, where “The White Band” has received a large number of prizes. All of this would be threatened if this law had not been passed. We were able to make a compromise last year and had to implement it. We ensure more transparency not only by the media authority, but also, and this is also important for the other media participants in the Austrian media market, by carrying out a so-called public value test on future new projects, new offers from the ORF, in other words, the added value for the population on the one hand, the impact on the media market, on the other, and to be taken into account, which will be implemented by the media authority, which will then be created when the law is adopted. We have foreseen that the Directorate-General will be reduced from the next period – this is another point that was very intensive in discussion – and many, many other small points in the ORF Act that have implications. And there was also something to fix the so-called dual broadcasting landscape, namely that private, on the one hand commercial, but also non-commercial, broadcasting operators, radio providers, television providers should also receive support. Last year we first created a media promotion for this area, at that time with € 5 million for the commercial and € 1 million for the non-commercial, and we foresee in this law that this will be tripled by 2013, i.e. from € 5 to € 15 and from € 1 million to € 3 million. – This is a very important point, which is also very important for the Austrian media landscape. ParlaMint-AT_2010-06-17-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00070_d8e7480	Next speaker: Mr Delegate Dr. Wittmann. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2010-06-17-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00070_d8e7502	Mr President. Mr. State Secretary! First of all, it has to be mentioned that this ORF law has been able to draw a final line under this debate at European level, since Austria is also fully implementing the Audiovisual Media Services Directive and can thus end this procedure, which has lasted since 2004. We have now also reached a quantum leap in the supervision process, because – and this is important to me as the chairman of the Constitutional Committee – we have created an independent media authority and this independent media authority has independent and independent supervision of the media – not only of the ORF, but also of all other media operators. And I believe that the procedure of the proposal, namely the proposal from the Federal Government and then confirmation in the main committee, ensures an objective selection. There is also a cooling-off phase for staff coming from media companies, so that there is no direct influence on the media authority, someone – a specialist – should have worked at a media company before. It has also been very important to me that this legal encroachment of an audit commission, which can give the company an economic mission, which it will then have to implement and for which the Supervisory Board would have to bear the responsibility, does not come – I would have thought this was a legal one, and I am glad that it has been amended by common agreement in the negotiations – but that this Audit Committee will be presented with a structural concept and will have to deliver an opinion on it, but in the end the Foundation Council still has the sole responsibility and not to fulfil a mandate from an authority and then to assume the responsibility for it. In financial terms, the following must be said: A company that has to finance one third of advertising, one third of fees and one third of the financial market, and loses € 280 million in the advertising market by opening advertising windows, cannot be expected to operate social assistance that the state has to operate. Mr Petzner, it is not about the ORF receiving a grant of €160 million, but the ORF grants free allowances to poor people, because the state actually wants them to be able to participate in the ORF, and this social assistance is recovered by the state. No company in the world would lose the benefits of social assistance! [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] These 160 million € are the refunding of this fee exemption! What I find completely incomprehensible is the following: You agree with this amendment, in which the 160 million € will be decided as a grant to the ORF, in the Committee – there is a five-party request! – and then put yourself here and pretend you weren't there. I recommend you get a grip on the archive of the ORF and borrow a film, namely, “... because they don’t know what they are doing.” That would fit with you, and I wouldn't compare you to James Dean. I would like to return to what was held back at that time. At that time one held: Subtitles can be displayed according to economic feasibility and technical feasibility. – That means there was in no way an obligation so far. And I would like to make you really aware of that and remind you of it. Then there was an increase: The exemption from the GIS charges for hearing impaired and visually impaired people has been abolished. This meant in the clear text now at the time: Imagine, for example, going to a bakery and buying ten semis, laying the money for ten semis and actually getting only the equivalent of a single semi. We have been able to follow an increase very slowly, and at the moment you can assume that you get about three and a half seconds for the value. The 2009 ORF report states that about 35 percent of the broadcast is subtitled. For blind and visually impaired people, it is noted that an audio descriptor. Maybe for you to explain: If a film dialogue is spoken, there is a clear description of the situation in the breaks between the dialogues, of the people who are there. That is, audio writing is only implemented quite, very little. In this case, there is in no way a 100% information. The target group we are now talking about is not a small target group. That is, barrier-free offer requires people with visual impairments, but also with hearing impairments. We are now talking about a group of half a million people. One could say, for example, that Austria is now almost final in Europe, even when compared to the Eastern countries, as far as the barrier-free offer is concerned. However, this law allows us to make a massive change in the situation so that Austria will suddenly be in the first third. That is, if this part of the law is now actually passed, then in the medium term – and this is set out in the law – 100 percent of the subtitles are planned. That is why I would like to thank my colleague Dieter Brosz very much. When they participate and sign a kind of gag contract, they sell all their rights to the ORF, even the rights to publish their own music on their homepage or elsewhere. This is not to be seen, and this is completely misleading, even as it is formulated in the invitation to tender. ParlaMint-AT_2010-06-17-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00070_d8e10024	We're coming to 3. Agenda item. First of all, Mr Magistrate. Stefan. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2010-06-17-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00070_d8e10048	Mr President. Mr. State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! It is the worst time, I think, to speak right after television time and after the vote. Nevertheless, it is an important issue. The Constitutional Court is important for all in this Republic, and we have found that on the one hand, the number of proceedings has increased – and on the other hand the duration of the proceedings – and we have discussed how to achieve an improvement in the sense of a shorter period of procedure. That is the content of our request. [[President Dr. Graf takes over the chairmanship.]] We have found that there should be a higher number of employees, of speakers, because they are very important for the preparation of decisions. And we have found that, in our opinion, it would be more useful, instead of the so-called session system, so that the Constitutional Court of Justice, for three weeks several times a year, would be able to introduce the permanent days of the Constitutional Court. This would ensure that procedures could be carried out more quickly. We have a procedure of up to six months in administrative law, and it would therefore be desirable to introduce or guarantee such a period of procedure in this area. But it's not supposed to be a compulsion. We have not required that, as in administrative law, the six months are necessarily introduced here, but it would be obvious and useful for the Constitutional Court to decide on an average within six months, or, if possible, within six months. For this, the permanent meeting of the Constitutional Court would be a useful contribution. It was used here that certain professions would be excluded because there are also constitutional judges who come from the professions. I do not think that is right. Firstly, it is not said that the constitutional judges must always be ordered on a lifetime basis, and secondly, someone from a free profession or also from the professorship could choose to become constitutional judges, even under these conditions, which would, of course, be useful. So this argument goes empty too. Our objective is, as has been said, to speed up procedures, and therefore this request. I hope you join him.

ParlaMint-AT_2010-06-17-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00070_d8e11609	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) The new maturity test at the AHS is intended to bring more quality and comparability. Today, in the bill, we also have the extension to the diploma and maturity examination of the higher schools. Three keywords are particularly important to me and us in this context: competence-oriented, partial-centred and mature-check for determining university maturity. Skill-oriented means that the tasks are determined throughout Austria, but the pupils of their school type are called for accordingly, i.e. that the trade students and the trade academics of their training are as required in their training as the HTL students and all the others in vocational training. Partly central for me is the political commitment to a joint expansion of school autonomy and a differentiated education offer in secondary education II. It stands for the recognition and appreciation of location-specific school profiles and for the diverse types of schools in a differentiated range of vocational training. The aim of the new maturity test is to encourage schools to further develop these priorities and make them the subject of final examinations. Individual benefits are to be comparable – therefore, not levelling down, but promoting and demanding benefits. These are the characteristics of the new mature test! In future, it will be important to also integrate the professional maturity test into this system in an adequate way. Thus, all three pillars – the AHS, the BHS and the teaching with the professional maturity test, i.e. the course with Matura – would have equivalent examinations despite different entrances. The third point relates to university maturity. In university maturity, it will become quite important that universities and universities support this model. They must therefore already be involved in the testing of the tasks and must say yes to the tasks, because this will also allow access to the university. Therefore, evaluation involving universities is a particularly important point. The best preparation for the university is pre-scientific work, diplomas and the subject-matter. These are already a major feature of vocational training. It is particularly important to me that we have jointly designed the proposal on this point and will now, I hope, decide together. The aim is to ensure that all forms of pre-scientific work are built on and developed on the many experiences of the last ten years, as the last ten years have shown that the BHS has taken an important step in the final work with its maturity and diploma examination. Evidence of professional skills has been carried out in cooperation with the economy. Many of these student projects have already led to innovations in small and medium-sized enterprises and in industry. We are talking about student patents – the legal steps are still to be taken – because many of our graduates have developed real, patent-ready innovations. I briefly mention the electronic blind stick as an example, a patent-ready student work. Research starts in very young years, so we also need a corresponding level. That's exactly what I'm saying as a science speaker. I am glad that we will come to this joint committee and plenary statement. The economy benefits from these joint accounts, and I am therefore particularly interested in integrating the regional economy into the final audits at regional level in future. In this sense, this is a first step in the right direction. It is now a matter of ensuring that implementation in its quality is achieved together.

ParlaMint-AT_2010-06-17-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00070_d8e15679	Mr President. Mrs Verkehrsminister! High house! The discussion about individual projects is typical of Austrian transport planning. Individual projects will be discussed, but never a whole. This does not really work, because whether the whole thing fits together or not ultimately also depends on the effect of individual projects. I think a tunnel, a railway tunnel can be useful under certain conditions, under other conditions, but also not. These conditions must be created through consistent holistic transport planning. It can be useful if it is not only invested line-by-line, it can also be expanded in terms of area, if real accessibility is improved, and especially when real relocation effects take place from road to rail. Just as this is happening now, there will be no such thing as the current transport plan is, as I have said, not a plan, but, as my colleague Gabi Moser put it, a wish programme of the federal states and not a truly targeted plan. Not only that every federal country has its own projects, not only that the road is still cheaper than the rail and therefore no relocation takes place, it is also invested in road projects in parallel, precisely at the S 36. I mean, how is the coral tunnel supposed to bring a discharge, bring a shift when the S 36, the S 37, is finished before anything can go into operation at all? I can also mention an example from Burgenland: the Styrian East Railway from Graz to Budapest, an existing railway infrastructure that would only have to be attracted to bring real relocation effects. But how does that work? However, it should also bring relief when, at the same time, a huge road project is being built or planned, namely the S7 and these road projects are becoming more and more of a priority than the rail projects that are expected to take place in parallel. In addition, there is massive resistance in the population both in S 36, S 37, S 7 and other road construction projects, massive fears, massive concerns about the environment and nature conservation. Above all, the huge projects, which often do not bring anything to the regional economy. I think we should already be aware of what transport planning really should be about, namely to ensure the mobility of people in Austria and to ensure the mobility of regional enterprises and not to satisfy the needs of the construction industry, but to really look at what the mobility needs of the people in Austria are. I believe that in the long term we can only satisfy the needs if there is a general rethinking in transport policy. 79 percent of the oil we import in Austria flows into traffic. Unfortunately, we experience and hear more and more dramatic news from the Gulf of Mexico. If we do not want to be guilty of such disasters – and I do not want to, we Greens do not want to do so – then we have to get out of this oil business. Transport policy has a major role to play in this respect. 79 percent of the oil coming to Austria is in traffic. That means the foreign exchange can only be: We need to change, we have to rethink and get rid of this oil dependence. Mrs Minister, if you are now making an evaluation, I think this is very positive. I believe that the road construction programme, this programme of wishes, should be seriously evaluated for its meaning. Since I am looking for you to take into account not only costs for your ministry, but also, we also need to save, not only voters and country elections, but in fact a general transport plan, a comprehensive transport concept to meet the needs of the population, in order to improve the quality of public transport, in terms of climate requirements and oil dependence. My wish would be that you would not only set priorities in such a way that certain projects will simply be postponed, postponed, postponed and ultimately everything will remain with the old, but that we will finally make courageous decisions, give no meaning to road projects, and actually take them out – I have already mentioned S 36, S 37, A 3 – and concentrate on meaningful projects, sensible transport planning. And by the way, I believe Austria needs a strong, independent and committed Ministry of the Environment. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2010-06-17-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00070_d8e16779	Mr President. Mrs. Minister! Another chapter in the history of the Education Documentation Act is written today, and it should not be mentioned that data protection measures have been put in place here, which we have criticized for years and which actually do not bring improvements. I recall that since the start of this measure, which is about collecting educational data – which we have nothing in principle to do even for longitudinal comparisons – the idea has always appeared and been implemented, which is usefully similar to the social security number, because it is easy to store on a data base all the data that do not have anything to do with social security, but in this case precisely with educational data. However, data links can cause corresponding problems. At the beginning it was still so that the parents were obliged to announce the number. If they have not done so, a procedure should actually be initiated to make the number known. In the meantime, we are so far away that we have turned this off and the carriers can also access the data directly and, so to speak, the parents can also be disabled if you want to have a decision to clarify whether this is legally acceptable at all. There is a counter-proposal, which has been on the table for years, namely a clear separate label for educational data, a personal label, which is not the social security number and on which one can simply collect educational data. Originally, the proposal for a law now relates to the fact that it also concerns the universities. You do both there now when you look at the facilities. There you now have your own registration number and the social security number. We believe that, at last, the recommendation of the Data Protection Council should also be taken into account, which meant that it is unfortunate that the social security number should continue to be used. From the point of view of the Data Protection Council – I quote – the use of sector-specific personal labels instead of social security would be preferable. This is still ignored by the Ministry, the social security number remains there. Therefore, I make the following request: [[Quoted print version removed]] This would be a step towards finally taking data protection in Austria more seriously to prevent data linkage and to prevent educational data from being stored over very long periods of time and possibly getting into the wrong hands of all the data abuses we have experienced lately. Mrs Minister, put a sign and present this bill to the National Council!

Firstly, I am against catalogues of various points of an authorization, because then in reality comes instead of the eighth point of the ninth, and then the legal gap is only pressing again. It also refers to the Minister's authorisation of regulation, which would have to be clarified and adopted with the main committee, so that before sending abroad, the legal certainty to which it is required is not properly re-established. I would therefore ask you to look at the draft in this regard. - Thank you.

It is extremely important that legal certainty can be guaranteed for the posted soldiers abroad. We doubt, however, that there is a need for a law of our own. In fact, it would be much more useful to change the already existing law on foreign employment. The Law on Foreign Action was passed under the leadership of the then Minister of Defence Herbert Scheibner in 2001, and it is a good law. The law on foreign use has always been an optimal legal basis for all previous Austrian federal forces, and it has always worked. The BZÖ is therefore convinced that a novel would create sufficient legal certainty. However, we are still ready for negotiation, ladies and gentlemen in the High House, because the Austrian federal army is celebrating 50 years of foreign operations and the military leadership is reporting the need. In these circumstances, we are also willing to create a law on foreign participation.

[[19:15:09]] 12. Point First reading: A request by Ursula Haubner, colleague and colleagues concerning a federal law with which the Federal Law of 25 January 1989 was adopted by the Court of Justice of the European Communities. In July 1962, private school (Private School Act) was amended (1040/A)
ParlaMint-AT_2010-06-17-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00070_d8e18631	We're on the 12th. Agenda item. We are going into the debate. I call Mrs Haubner. 3 minutes speaking time. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2010-06-17-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00070_d8e18658	Mr President. High house! Our request concerns the situation of private schools. We have in Austria a wide range of services, in the diversity of the school landscape, so-called non-confessional schools in free circulation and we have religious private schools. These are offers for the parents, for the children who are important but who must also be so expanded that everyone who wants to have the opportunity to attend such a school. The problem is financing, which is very different in terms of conventional private schools and non-confessional schools. Confessional private schools automatically receive the replacement of teachers' salaries, non-confessional schools free of charge receive subsidies and support, and there are also differences again. Those who are members of the EFFE Austria, these are four associations, get relatively much, namely € 4.2 million last year. There are 13 Austrian Waldorf schools that get €2.7 million, then there are 42 network schools that get €1.2 million and then there are five Montessori schools, which now get around €231 000 from the federal government. – So you see, there is a lot of money being invested, but still it is not satisfactory because it is ultimately at the discretion of the respective minister, the respective minister. Secondly, there is a special requirement profile for inspectors, inspectors who inspect these schools and the respective educational work. There is also absolute need for rethinking. These are two important topics that we should discuss very intensively and also with experts in order to achieve better creation and maximum transparency. We have a subcommittee next week, in which the private school law and the problem of private schools as a whole can be discussed. There, perhaps some light will come into the dark. I am already looking forward to the discussion, but above all to the fact that we are moving forward in this context. - Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_2010-06-17-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00070_d8e18698	Next speaker: Mr Mayer. – 3 minutes speaking time. - Please.

However, you also have to take away the illusion: It will not be possible to promote all non-public schools as well as public schools, but one must find a means. It is not meant to stifle the initiatives. The Minister has already done a lot in the last two or three years. We've doubled the money for that. That's not nothing, but you should keep talking about it. In this context, the challenge of making the public school system so open is also so open that the need does not become so strong as to turn away from public schools and seek private niches. That is why we are well advised if we treat these initiatives fairly, discuss them with them, seek discourse, but at the same time make every effort to make public education as attractive as we really want to do, so that the problem does not arise from this side either. These students have started the school year in the assumption – and not in the certainty – that they are in a school with public law that they receive certificates. And they are now, a few weeks before they finish school, being presented with the fact that they have practically only received home lessons. That's not how that's supposed to be! We must at least give this legal certainty to the parents who take on this great financial burden. This seems to me to be a very central point of this proposal. Let us therefore strengthen public education by giving legal certainty to private schools! Let us take advantage of what we have in this area, namely innovative methods, reform pedagogical approaches, some of which do not exist in public schools, but which we can take into consideration in public schools! That is the great advantage, that is what non-confessional private schools are currently offering us, and that is why they deserve the appropriate support. Thank you.
